## M32 round result (round 28, 2026-11-16 11:55, after the data)

Spec `tools/m32_spec.md` (10:10, before any measurement).  Node 2140760 (A100-PCIE-40GB, ¥2.80/h),
rented 10:07, released 11:50.  Verdict: **`colour_residual_is_bits`** (J1 FAIL, J2 PASS, J4 PASS with the
corrected metric, J5 PASS).

Question: M31/N38 located the near-lossless residual (0.46/0.63/0.98 dB) in the colour block; is it a
rank deficiency or a bit deficiency?  Four plans hold the raw colour budget at 48 bits and trade subspace
for precision, plus one rate control at rank 16.

| plan | bicycle (76.56 MB ctrl) | stump (62.59 MB) | bonsai (15.30 MB) |
|---|---|---|---|
| r16_b48 | 24.06 | 25.52 | 26.70 |
| r24_b48 (iso-bytes) | 23.37 (-0.69) | 24.51 (-1.01) | 25.84 (-0.86) |
| r32_b48 | 22.63 (-1.43) | 23.74 (-1.78) | 24.79 (-1.91) |
| r8_b48 | 23.65 (-0.41) | 25.12 (-0.40) | 26.72 (+0.02) |
| r16_b64 (+10.0/9.8/12.3 % bytes) | 24.22 (+0.16) | 25.68 (+0.16) | 27.02 (+0.32) |

Rank is not the constraint: every same-byte widening loses, and it loses *more* on the scene with the
largest residual, which is the opposite of the rank-deficiency prediction.  Sixteen more bits inside the
same sixteen dimensions is the only move that pays, and it pays 0.16-0.32 dB for ~10-12 % of the file.

Two mechanisms worth keeping:

* **iso-raw is not iso-file**: all four 48-bit plans write 36.79 MB of coefficient codes on bicycle, which
  zstd turns into 18.58/20.08/22.01/29.25 MB -- 6-bit codes (64 near-uniform symbols) compress far worse
  than 3-bit codes (8 skewed symbols).  M20/M24's alphabet effect, arriving from the allocation side.
* **the offline selector works only if it charges truncation**: coefficient error measured inside the
  retained subspace ranks r8 first on all three scenes (Spearman -0.70/-0.70/-0.90 vs PSNR), because a
  rank-8 fit is never scored on the 37 of 45 columns it discarded.  Measured against the original 45
  columns the ordering is exact on all three scenes (Spearman -1.000): r16_b64 28.7/35.8/35.5 % < r16_b48
  50.7/55.5/55.4 < r8 54.8/59.8/51.9 < r24 86.9/105.0/93.6 < r32 119.5/136.6/131.7.

Cross-node note (recorded, not resolved): the bonsai control renders 26.70 dB here and 27.08 dB in M31
for a same-size container, while bicycle reproduced to the digit (24.06 dB, SSIM 0.7393) in the same
pair of rounds.  Re-encoding on this node reproduces the container byte for byte (md5 a3d3488a...), so
the encoder is deterministic per build; the two nodes provably did not share a rasteriser build (this
image shipped without gsplat/scikit-image/lpips and needed a hand install).  All M32 claims are
within-round deltas; bonsai absolute PSNR carries ~0.4 dB of build uncertainty.

Implementation kept: `experiments/run_m32.sh` (setup|encode|coef|render|all), `tools/m32_coef_error.py`
(per-plan quantiser error + the full-column metric), `tools/m32_collect.py`, `tools/m32_analyse.py`
(tables, J1/J2, Spearman), `tools/m32_{drive,render_all}.sh`, `tools/fetch_m32.ps1`.  Decoder unchanged.
Gate: **93 tests passed**, 881 byte cases 0 mismatches, 144/144 py3.11.

Docs: `experiments/M32_RESULTS.md`, appendix **N39**, `FRONTIER_BOUND.md` §11, `sota_table.md`
colour-block + offline-selector tables.

Cost: 1.72 h * ¥2.80 = ¥4.81.

## M33 round result (round 29, 2026-11-16, RTX A4000 2140862, ¥1.35/h)

**Question.** The goal asks for every dimension, and robustness is the one this series has only ever
measured mechanically (M19: a burst kills one frame, positions do not move).  What PSNR does a damaged
file *render* at, and does the container's neutral-fill rule (M21) survive contact with the M22 colour
block?

**Pre-registration** `tools/m33_spec.md` (12:05, before the first corrupted byte): L1 1 % burst within
2.0 dB of intact on all three scenes; L2 PSNR non-increasing in damage and >= 18.0 dB at 5 %; L3 a 50 %
prefix renders >= 20.0 dB; L4 a damaged decode stays within 1.2x of the intact time and peak RSS.
Schedule fixed in advance: burst 0.10/0.25/0.50/1/2/5 %, prefixes 25/50/75/90 %, three scenes, one
render per condition.

**Result: `degrades_but_visibly`.**  L2 and L4 pass; L1 fails on bonsai alone; L3 fails on all three.

| condition | bicycle 76.56 MB / 24.06 | stump 62.59 MB / 25.52 | bonsai 15.30 MB / 27.08 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.10 % burst | 23.37 (-0.69) | 24.67 (-0.85) | 23.44 (-3.64) |
| 0.25 / 0.50 / 1.0 % | 23.37 | 24.67 | 23.44 |
| 2.0 % | 22.76 (-1.30) | 24.67 | 23.44 |
| 5.0 % | 22.26 (-1.80) | 24.25 (-1.27) | 23.44 |
| prefix 25 / 50 % | 9.25 / 9.25 | 11.27 / 11.27 | 8.87 / 8.87 |
| prefix 75 % | 17.22 | 16.05 | 10.29 |
| prefix 90 % | 22.71 | 23.31 | 25.53 |

Damaged decode on bicycle: 11.28 s / 2.08 GB against 11.28 s / 2.01 GB intact (L4 ratio 1.00 / 1.03).
Geometry never moves: means/scales/opacities/quats are byte-identical to the control in all 33
mechanical reports.

**Two decoder defects, both found by this round, both fixed.**
1. `read_container(tolerant=True)` appended nothing for a frame that failed its CRC and padded the
   stream at the end -> every later byte in that stream was reinterpreted: one 4 MB frame in `sh_coef`
   changed the colour of all 6,131,954 primitives (median |d| 0.33).  Fixed by keeping the hole's
   length (`b"\x00" * raw_frames[i]`) and counting it in `meta["damaged_bytes"]`.
2. The M22 basis block had no neutral symbol: zero bytes decode to `(0 + 0.5) * step - 1`, the most
   negative coefficient in the alphabet, so a lost frame painted 72.6 % of bicycle as dark as the model
   can go (16.51 dB).  Its reserved code is the midpoint `2**(b-1)` per component;
   `m22_basis.neutralise_coef` maps each lost byte range to the component and primitive window it
   covers (the codes are concatenated component-major).  With it the same burst costs 0.69 dB.

The three numbers for one condition — 19.80 before, 16.51 after the length fix, 23.37 after both — are
the round's real result: the first bug had been hiding the second, and the middle number is the one
that measured the format.

**Why L3 fails, measured rather than argued.**  Geometry is 70 % of the payload; a renderable model
needs all of it plus DC and its level (~57 MB = 75 % of the file).  `tools/m33_reorder.py` rewrote the
same streams with colour first (`streams_identical=True`) and re-ran the ladder: 50 % still 9.25 dB.
**The order is not the constraint, the dependency set is** — below 75 % a zero scale covers no pixels,
so the prefix is empty rather than coarse.  Moving the ladder needs a coarse *predictor* for geometry
in the first half of the file, which is a different format.

**Frame granularity is the real robustness parameter.**  A 4 MB frame inside an 18.6 MB component-major
block covers 68 % of the primitives of one component (hence 72.6 % "changed" for 0.69 dB); bonsai's
whole colour block is one frame, so its smallest possible corruption is its entire refinement (-3.64).
Interleaving the codes primitive-major would put ~0.7 M primitives behind the same frame, but M20
measured that layout 17 % worse for compression: the shipping choice buys bytes with damage
granularity, and both sides are now measured.

**Implementation kept**: `tools/m33_damage.py` (schedule + mechanical report + render),
`tools/m33_collect.py`, `tools/m33_mech.py`, `tools/m33_probe.py`, `tools/m33_reorder.py` (DC-first
rewrite), `tools/m33_cost.py` (per-file decode time and peak RSS, subprocess-isolated),
`tools/m33_{prep,go,redamage,dcf,status,tables}.sh`, `experiments/run_m33.sh`,
`tools/fetch_m33.ps1`, tests `test_m33_damage.py` (2) and `test_m33_basis_neutral.py` (2).
Gate: **99 tests passed**, 881 byte cases 0 mismatches, `m19_codec --selftest` ALL PASS.

Docs: `experiments/M33_RESULTS.md`, appendix **N40**, `FRONTIER_BOUND.md` §12, `sota_table.md`
robustness table.

Cost: 3.5 h * ¥1.35 = ¥4.7, of which ~40 min was a re-fetched COLMAP tree (space-separated `SCENES` is
parsed as a comma-separated list, so the first pass silently fetched 114 of bicycle's 194 images).

## M34 round result (round 30, 2026-11-16 18:55, after the data)

Pre-registered `tools/m34_spec.md` (15:35). Three amendments before the first build: `MAX_FRAMES` 64 → 512
(otherwise a 256 kB request is silently floored at `raw/64`), the models come from the INRIA archive
instead of `hf-mirror` (which swung 63 → 614 → 22 kB/s inside an hour), and the measurement is cut to
**bonsai** because bicycle's 1.52 GB did not download in a sane round.

**Measured** (bonsai, 1,244,819 prims, 16 views, shipping configuration unchanged, all three arms decode
to the identical model sha1 `ebe2cdd08c2d`):

| `MIN_FRAME` | file | vs 4 MB | `sh_coef` frames | decode | RSS | intact | 0.10 % burst | 1.0 % | 5.0 % |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4 MB | 15,302,362 B | — | 2 | 1.63 s | 0.66 GB | 27.06 | **23.97 (−3.09)** | −3.09 | −3.09 |
| 1 MB | 15,352,658 B | +0.36 % | 8 | 1.63 s | 0.66 GB | 27.06 | **25.78 (−1.28)** | −1.28 | 24.79 (−2.27) |
| 256 KB | 15,297,691 B | −0.03 % | 29 | 1.66 s | 0.66 GB | 27.06 | **26.92 (−0.14)** | 25.62 (−1.44) | 25.43 (−1.63) |

Criteria: **P1 PASS** (+0.36 % / −0.03 %), **P2 PASS** (26.92 ≥ 26.0), **P3 not measured** (bicycle and
stump never landed), **P4 PASS** (1.02× / 1.00×). Verdict **`smaller_frames_win`**: the smallest
detectable damage drops 3.09 → 0.14 dB, 22×, for 0.03 % fewer bytes.

Mechanism: the cost of a lost frame tracks the fraction of the colour block it covers (96 % → −3.09,
24 % → −1.28, 6 % → −0.14), not the byte count — which is exactly why M33's L1 failed on bonsai (4.19 MB
colour block, 4 MB frame) and not on bicycle. Smaller frames are free here because a frame header is 64 B
and more, smaller zstd frames compress as well or better on a 15 MB file.

Docs: `experiments/M34_RESULTS.md`, appendix **N41** (setup was 90 % of the round; a truncated
`images.bin` reported success), `FRONTIER_BOUND.md` §13, `sota_table.md` robustness row.

Bugs fixed on the way: `experiments/fetch_m360_colmap.sh` now verifies every COLMAP sparse file against
the repository's own size (the mirror returned 19.9 MB of a 68.4 MB `images.bin`, which parses a valid
header and dies 200 records in); `m19_codec.MAX_FRAMES` 64 → 512; `m22_basis` coefficient layout
`interleaved` (implemented, tested, deliberately not measured — error energy is conserved by that
permutation).

Gate: **104 tests passed**, 881 byte cases 0 mismatches, `m19_codec --selftest` ALL PASS.

Cost: node 2141031 (V100-16GB, ¥1.85/h) 15:29 → 18:55 ≈ 3.4 h ≈ **¥6.3**, of which ~20 min was
measurement; the rest was the download documented in N41.

Next: bicycle/stump still owe P3, and the models are now cached in `/mnt/m34cache` so that check is a
five-minute round rather than a five-hour one.

## M35 round result (round 31, 2026-11-16 21:15, after the data)

**Branch `rd_map_complete`** with Q1's monotonicity clause recorded as failed. Node 2141179 (A100-PCIE-40GB,
¥2.80/h, 19:00–21:15, ≈¥6.3), released.

- **The map** (bicycle, 6,131,954 prims, clean 24.52 dB): 76.56 MB/18.90×/24.06 · 69.34/20.87×/23.80 ·
  66.24/21.85×/23.60 · 57.23/25.28×/22.53 · 55.18/26.22×/21.82; **dominated**: 69.30/20.88×/22.82.
  stump (4,961,797 prims, clean 26.15): 62.59/18.71×/25.52 · 56.72/20.65×/25.30 · 54.41/21.52×/25.04 ·
  47.46/24.67×/24.42 · 45.75/25.60×/22.32; dominated 56.77/20.63×/22.70.
- **Fit over the four sh3 points**: bicycle +3.74 dB per doubling (R² 0.942), crosses 23.0 dB at 61.1 MB and
  24.0 dB at 73.5 MB; stump +2.83 dB per doubling (R² 0.971), 33.2 MB and 42.3 MB.
- **Floor**: 45–55 MB at fixed primitive count; the cheapest point is 9.00 B/prim with 7.97 B geometry.
  Mid-rate budgets (HAC 27.54 MB, PCGS 19.09 MB) need fewer primitives, not fewer bits.
- **SH degree is dominated**: `gp6-6-6_c6_sh2` sits within 0.06 % (bicycle) / 0.09 % (stump) of a sh3
  container's bytes and 1.02 / 2.60 dB below it — the worst byte-saving axis measured so far.
- **M34 P3, on bicycle**: 256 KB at 1.0 % burst = **23.73 dB** (≥23.5 ✓), 4 MB = 23.45 dB at every fraction
  up to ~5 %; 256 KB costs **+0.05 %** bytes (Q3 ✓). Stump P3 not measured (COLMAP sparse layout, fixed at
  20:54, no time for a second arm).
- **Cache (Q4 ✓)**: `/mnt/m35cache` holds bicycle 1,520,726,124 B `64d357cb…`, stump 1,230,527,188 B
  `e9186b7a…`, bonsai 308,716,644 B `a16af6d8…`; hashes in `artifacts/a4/m35/m35_cache.json`.
- **Mirror**: `hf-mirror.com` whole-file GET **~10 MB/s**, INRIA zip by 12 range workers **0.2 MB/s**, and
  `hf-mirror` range requests return ~0.6 kB/s (it ignores `Range`). M34's range downloader was the wrong
  tool for this host.
- **Two documentation errors in our own tooling**: `_c<N>` is the cluster-position width (floor 6), not the
  DC width — cost one ladder rung and two amendments; `m33_damage --burst` takes fractions, so the first
  arm measured 10 %/100 % instead of 0.1 %/1.0 %.
- Docs: `experiments/M35_RESULTS.md`, appendix **N42**, `FRONTIER_BOUND.md` **§14**, `tools/m35_spec.md`
  (+ two amendments), `.gitignore`-free artifacts under `experiments/artifacts/a4/m35/`.

**Next round** should spend the cached models: the bicycle/stump cache removes the 40-minute setup that has
bounded every round since M34, which makes a training experiment (rate-aware fine-tuning, or a pruned model
at 30 % keep) affordable for the first time.

## M36 round result (round 32, 2026-11-16 23:00, after the data)

Pre-registered in `tools/m36_spec.md` at 21:25, one sensitivity arm added in `tools/m36_amend.md` at 22:38
(before arm d existed on any node). Node 2141303, A100-PCIE-40GB ¥2.80/h, ~2.2 h ≈ ¥6.2.

**Setup.** Source = published bicycle pruned to 25 % by volume (1,532,988 primitives, 380.2 MB, 15.18 dB
before fine-tuning — M26's source, reproduced: prune gives exactly 1,532,988 / 6,131,954). Recipe = 12k
steps, factor 4, `--no_densify --no_scene_scale --lr_scale 0.3 --grad_clip 1.0 --split interleaved
--gt-convention archive --keep_all_views`. Pressure = `mean(log2(1+(θ/σ)²))` on `shN, sh0, scales,
opacities`, σ = 1.5 × the group's median at init (shN 0.0437, sh0 1.70, scales 6.78, opacities 7.21), λ
calibrated at step 0 to a 5 % gradient share and frozen.

| arm | loss | λ | container | B/prim | PSNR | SSIM | wall |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| a | plain (M17 recipe) | — | 19,272,155 B | 12.574 | 21.07 | 0.5166 | 731 s |
| **b** | + log rate term, 5 % | 0.9319 | **17,959,298 B** | **11.715** | **21.24** | 0.5279 | 812 s |
| c | + L2 control, 5 % | 0.2959 | 18,381,419 B | 11.990 | 21.19 | 0.5281 | 780 s |
| d | + log rate term, 15 % | 2.796 | 17,482,803 B | 11.404 | 21.09 | 0.5200 | 823 s |

Arm a reproduces M26's A arm (`[eval] cal=21.42` vs 21.40), so the pipeline is unchanged.

**Verdicts.** R1 FAIL literally (0.932× bytes needs ≤0.85; +0.17 dB needs ≥0.5), but **b Pareto-dominates a**
— 6.8 % fewer bytes, +0.17 dB, better SSIM, 1.11× the wall clock. R2 FAIL: the L2 control at the same
gradient share is within 0.05 dB and 2.4 % of the log arm, so the entropy shape is not measurable here; what
pays is shrinkage towards the quantiser grid. R3 PASS (1.11×), R4 PASS (9 tests). Sensitivity: the knee is
below a 15 % share (d buys 2.7 % more bytes for 0.15 dB). Derived label, flagged as post-hoc:
**`pressure_pays_through_shrinkage`**.

**What it changes.** The mid-rate point becomes **17.96 MB / 21.24 dB** (from 19.27 MB / 21.07 dB; M26's
factor-4 point was 24.96 MB / 21.24 dB on the M24 container). Against HAC 27.54 MB / 25.05 dB and PCGS
19.09 MB / 25.12 dB the gap is unchanged at 3.9 dB — a 0.17 dB gain cannot close it, and the loss was never
where the gap lived.

**Two failures worth keeping** (`APPENDIX_negative_results.md` N43). (1) R1 was written as a trade, so a
Pareto win scored as a failure; every rate-distortion criterion needs a dominance clause. (2) A control must
be calibrated to the same *pressure*, not the same weight: b's λ was 0.93 and c's 0.30 for the same 5 %
gradient share, and an equal-λ control would have been three times weaker than the treatment.

**Two device bugs cost ~10 min** (both fixed, both now in docstrings and tests): `torch.quantile` needs its
`q` tensor on the parameter's device, and it refuses tensors over ~16.7 M elements — the SH rest group is
69 M, so σ subsamples to 4 M elements first.

**Next round** (M37, already pre-registered in `tools/m37_spec.md` at 23:05 and running on the same node):
the same pressure on the *full* 6.13 M model, where the headline number lives — does a 5 % share buy bytes
when there is no pruned headroom to recover?

## M37 round result (round 33, 2026-11-17 00:12, after the data)

Pre-registered `tools/m37_spec.md` at 23:05, on the same node (2141303, A100 ¥2.80/h, released 00:12;
M36+M37 together ≈ 2.95 h ≈ ¥8.3).

**Setup.** Same recipe as M36 but initialised from the published 6,131,954-primitive bicycle model instead of
the 25 %-pruned one: 12k steps, factor 4, `--no_densify --no_scene_scale --lr_scale 0.3 --grad_clip 1.0
--split interleaved --gt-convention archive --keep_all_views`, container `gp6-6-6_c8_sh3` (rank-16/48-bit
basis), 25 held-out views. λ calibrated at step 0 to a 5 % gradient share: **0.2358** (vs 0.9319 on the pruned
model — a converged model already sits near the scale the penalty wants).

| arm | loss | λ | container | B/prim | PSNR | SSIM | wall |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| a | plain | — | 76,931,227 B | 12.546 | 23.69 | 0.7244 | 1265 s |
| **b** | + log rate term, 5 % share | 0.2358 | **73,124,530 B** | **11.925** | **23.81** | 0.7331 | 1577 s |
| reference | untouched published model | — | 76,556,000 B | 12.480 | **24.06** | 0.7393 | — |

**Verdicts.** K1 PASS by its second clause (+0.12 dB at 0.951× bytes; the first clause misses by 0.0006 in
byte ratio, recorded as a near miss rather than rounded). K2 PASS (bytes down, SSIM +0.0087). K3 PASS
(1.25×). Branch `pressure_helps_near_lossless`.

**But both arms are dominated.** Fine-tuning a converged model for 12k steps at factor 4 costs 0.37 dB and
0.4 MB; the pressure recovers 0.12 dB and 4.9 % of the bytes, and b's 73.12 MB / 23.81 dB is below M35's own
curve at that size (≈ 23.94 dB by interpolation). So the shipping point stays **76.56 MB / 18.90× / 24.06 dB**
with no fine-tuning.

**Bracketed conclusion (M36 + M37).** The rate pressure is a **repair tool, not a compressor**: on the pruned
model (15.18 dB start, ample headroom) it beats its control beyond parity; on the converged model it only
pays back part of the damage the schedule itself did. Consequence for future rounds: on this frontier
re-encoding is the cheap knob (0.26 dB per 9.4 % bytes, M35) and retraining is not (0.37 dB per 0.4 %, M37) —
any "train harder" idea must beat a free knob.

**Docs**: `experiments/M37_RESULTS.md`, appendix **N44**, `FRONTIER_BOUND.md` **§16**, `sota_table.md`
mid-rate section already carries the M36 row (M37 adds no frontier point, so the table is unchanged).

**Next round.** Two levers remain untried and both are cheap with the models cached on `/mnt`:
(a) the pressure on a *fresh* 6.13M-primitive training run (not a fine-tune), where the schedule damage and
the pressure act together rather than in sequence — the only configuration in which HAC/PCGS-like results are
even possible; (b) a 30 %-keep prune with the pressure, to map where between 25 % and 100 % the repair stops
paying and the frontier point moves.

## M38 round result (round 34, 2026-11-17 01:20, after the data)

Pre-registered `tools/m38_spec.md` at 00:20 (node 2141436, A100 ¥2.80/h, rented 00:19 released 01:20 ≈ ¥2.9).

**Question.** M29 showed the geometric codes are at their entropy, so M38 tried the last thing left on that
axis: one index into a joint 8-D codebook `(log σxyz, qwxyz, logit α)` instead of four scalar groups
(44 bits/primitive).

| k | container | B/prim | PSNR | SSIM | codebook |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4096 | 54,606,955 B | 8.905 | 19.58 | 0.5149 | 65,536 B |
| 16384 | 56,338,847 B | 9.188 | 19.61 | 0.5270 | 262,144 B |
| 65536 | 58,598,006 B | 9.556 | 20.36 | 0.5587 | 1,048,576 B |
| scalar baseline | 76,556,000 B | 12.480 | **24.06** | 0.7393 | — |

**Verdict `scalar_is_already_better`.** L1 FAIL (20.36 vs ≥24.00 dB); L2 moot (codebook ≤1 MB, distortion is
the cost); L3 PASS (encode 166–174 s, decode/render unchanged); L4 PASS (codebook counted, scalar streams
removed — a test asserts it).

**The decisive number is at equal bytes**: M35's curve gives ≈22.6 dB at 58.6 MB, so the best codebook is
**2.2 dB below the frontier** at its own size. Mechanically: 16× more centroids bought 0.78 dB for 4 MB, and
the residual is dominated by log-scale error (RMSE 0.28 in log units = 32 % scale error).

**What it closes.** Together with M29 this ends the family "fewer symbols per primitive": scalar codes are at
entropy, joint codes are worse at any affordable size. The bytes live in the *number* of primitives, which is
what M26's prune-and-fine-tune exploits and what anchors/context models over fewer, better-placed Gaussians
achieve in HAC/PCGS.

**Next round.** Two options, both cheap with the `/mnt` cache: (a) push the prune-and-fine-tune prefix further
down — 15 % and 10 % keep with the M36 pressure, to find where the mid-rate frontier actually turns; (b) a
decode-side claim that needs no new model: measure end-to-end time-to-first-frame and random-access cost of
the shipping container on all three scenes, which is the one dimension of the objective still reported from a
single scene.

## M39 round result (round 22, 2026-11-17 02:50, after the data)

Pre-registered in `tools/m39_spec.md` before any measurement: prune to 15 % and 10 %, fine-tune each with (b)
and without (a) the rate pressure, encode at the shipping spec, render on the 25 held-out views.

**Verdict `pressure_does_not_scale_with_headroom`.** N1 PASS at both prefixes (b/a = 0.9412×, +0.100 dB at
15 %; 0.9455×, +0.020 dB at 10 %). N2 PASS under the Pareto reading — `p15-b` and `p10-b` are Pareto-optimal
against every measured point, extending the curve to 7.24 MB = 199.80× at 19.02 dB; the pre-registered
interpolation clause is undefined below 17.96 MB, and the first draft of the judge clamped there and invented
a dominance (fixed, with a test). N3/N4 PASS.

The hypothesis was wrong in a useful way: the byte saving is nearly constant (−6.8 / −5.9 / −5.5 % at 25 / 15
/ 10 % keep) while the dB gain falls (+0.17 / +0.10 / +0.02). The pressure is a fixed-rate lever worth about
half a byte per primitive, not a quality-recovery mechanism. Container cost stays ~12.5 B/primitive at every
prefix.

Files: `experiments/M39_RESULTS.md`, appendix N46, `FRONTIER_BOUND.md` §18, `sota_table.md` (extreme-rate
rows), `experiments/artifacts/a4/m39_*.{txt,log,json}`. Code: `experiments/run_m39.sh`, `tools/m39_table.py`,
`experiments/tests/test_m39_frontier.py`. Cost ≈ ¥4.0 (node 2141461, 1.4 h).

## M40 round result (round 23, 2026-11-17 04:20, after the data)

Pre-registered in `tools/m40_spec.md`: measure 4 MB vs 256 KB frames on three scenes (bytes, decode time, peak
RSS, 0.1 % and 1.0 % burst), then adopt the finer boundary if it is free.

**Verdict `the_damage_win_is_scene_specific`** — T1 PASS (byte price +0.053 % / +0.093 % / −0.031 %), T2 PASS
(decode 7.88 / 6.38 / 1.57 s, RSS 1.96 / 1.60 / 0.47 GB), T3 FAIL as written (+0.55 / +0.66 / +3.29 dB at
0.1 %, and the bonsai 1 % floor was mis-copied), T4 PASS. The property that holds on all three scenes is a
bound: with 256 KB frames a 0.1 % burst costs ≤ 0.35 dB, with 4 MB frames it costs 0.61–3.64 dB depending on
how much of the colour block one frame covers.

**Default changed:** `m19_codec.MIN_FRAME` 4 MB → 256 KB, `encode_model` records `meta["min_frame"]`, new test

## M41 round result (round 24, 2026-11-17 04:45, after the data)

Pre-registered in `tools/m41_spec.md`: compress the container's frames on a pool, require byte identity at every
worker count, and measure the write's wall clock, RSS and md5 on three scenes.

**Verdict `parallel_encode_wins`.** E1 PASS (md5 identical at N = 1,2,4,8 on bicycle/stump/bonsai; the files are
the M40 containers to the byte), E2 PASS (bicycle jobs=4 = 0.284× of jobs=1; jobs=8 = 0.167×), E3 PASS (same
file, tensors compared), E4 PASS (RSS 1.00×).

The honest denominator is the round's real result: the write is 11.31 s of a 70.2 s encode on bicycle, so end to
end the pool buys 1.13× (stump 1.04×, bonsai 1.34×). Stream construction is 84–94 % of encode time. The table
also exposed peak encoder RSS of 18.8 GB (bicycle) and a build time that is *not* proportional to the primitive
count (58.9 s at 6.13 M vs 137.7 s at 4.96 M, because `m24_alpha.choose()` compresses the SH candidates twice
before the winner is compressed a third time).

Files: `experiments/M41_RESULTS.md`, appendix N48, `FRONTIER_BOUND.md` §20, `sota_table.md` encode row,
`experiments/artifacts/a4/m41_*.json`. Code: `experiments/m41_par.py`, `experiments/run_m41.sh`,
`write_container(..., jobs)`, `experiments/tests/test_m41_parallel.py`. Cost ≈ ¥1.4.

## M42 round result (round 25, 2026-11-17 05:20, after the data)

Pre-registered in `tools/m42_spec.md`: probe the encoder stage by stage, then remove the top memory consumer with
changes that are byte-neutral by construction.

**Verdict `cheaper_but_not_enough`** (label chosen after the data; the pre-registered branch names do not fit).
The probe put the 18.8 GB peak inside `m4_bytes._encode` — four float64 temporaries in
`codes_published_sh_parts` and an `int64` plane array in `pack_bits`. Both now slice (per-row quantisation, whole
bytes per chunk) and the A/B on one node gives, old → new: bicycle 190.1 → 150.1 s and 18.38 → 11.63 GB; stump
127.2 → 103.9 s and 14.94 → 9.51 GB; bonsai 21.4 → 19.9 s and 4.07 → 2.78 GB. **The containers are md5-identical
to the M40/M41 files on all three scenes** (L3 PASS, L4 PASS).

**L1 FAIL** (11.63 GB vs a 6.0 GB target), **L2 FAIL** (150 s vs 100 s). What remains is live data: float64
attribute arrays 4.5 GB (SH rest 2.21 GB), an `int64` code array 2.21 GB where uint8 would do, and three layout
candidates alive at once. Also recorded: the same code path measured 58.9 s on M41's host and 150.1 s here — a
2.5× cross-host spread, so encode-time claims must be same-node A/B.

Files: `experiments/M42_RESULTS.md`, appendix N49, `FRONTIER_BOUND.md` §21, `sota_table.md` encoder row,
`experiments/artifacts/a4/m42_*`. Code: `m4_bytes.codes_published_sh_parts` / `pack_bits` (sliced),
`tools/m42_mem.py`, `tools/m42_encode.py --legacy`, `experiments/tests/test_m42_encoder_mem.py`. Cost ≈ ¥1.7.

## M43 / M44 round results (rounds 26–27, 2026-11-17 06:00, after the data)

**M43 — `narrowing_is_not_enough`.** Narrowed SH codes to `uint8`/`uint16` and freed the rejected layout candidates
before the basis fit. Peak 11.66 → 11.65 GB (bicycle), 9.52 → 9.49 (stump), 2.92 → 2.70 (bonsai): the criterion (6.0 GB)
failed and the peak did not move. Build improved (0.934×, 0.575×, 0.945×). Containers byte-identical.

**M44 — `the_peak_had_moved`.** Stage probe on the M43 build: `current` never above 7.2 GB; peak 11.93 GB, all of it a
window inside `encode_layout`. The largest thing in that window was `take(block) = block[perm]`, a second full-size
float64 copy of the SH rest block. The quantiser now gathers `flat[perm[i:i+chunk]]` per slice, so `_encode` never
materialises the permutation. Peak 11.65 → **9.60 GB** (bicycle), 9.49 → 7.83, 2.70 → 2.27; build 126.9 → 108.9 s;
md5 identical on all three scenes. M1 met at the boundary (9.60 ≤ 9.6, predicted 9.45), M2 0.858×, M3/M4 pass.

**Where the remaining 9.60 GB is, measured not guessed.** Live footprint 7.2 GB: float64 attribute arrays 4.5 GB (SH
rest 2.21 GB), int64 position codes + cluster header 0.5 GB, candidate stream sets 0.5 GB (removed in M43), plus a
4.3 GB `load_model` spike on a 1.52 GB PLY. The rest block is float64 because `m22_basis.fit` runs `svd_lowrank` on it
and that rounding decides the file ⇒ **6 GB is unreachable by memory work**; it needs a streaming float64 fit or an
explicit byte-identity decision. That question is the next round's, and it must be answered before it is coded.

**Files**: `experiments/M43_RESULTS.md` (both rounds), `APPENDIX_negative_results.md` **N50**, `FRONTIER_BOUND.md`
**§22**, `sota_table.md` encoder table, `tools/m43_spec.md`, `tools/m44_spec.md`, `tools/m43_ab.py` (four variants,
`--variant legacy|m42|m43|m44`), `tools/m43_table.py`, `experiments/run_m43.sh`, `experiments/run_m44.sh`,
`experiments/tests/test_m43_narrow_codes.py` (4), `test_m44_gather.py` (3).
**Gates**: 145 tests, 881 byte cases 0 mismatches. **Cost**: node 2141496, A100-PCIE-40GB ¥2.80/h, 1.0 h ≈ ¥2.8.

**`the_pool_costs_more_than_it_saves`** — the two colour alphabets are independent, so `build(..., jobs>1)` runs them

## M48 round result (round 31, 2026-11-17 10:10, after the data)

**`saliency_prune_is_a_method`** — M47's single-scene, single-seed result replicated on stump and bonsai plus a second
bicycle seed. One node, one session, protocol fixed before the data, nothing tuned per scene:

| scene | volume (equal bytes) | saliency (equal bytes) | Δ |
|---|---|---|---|
| bicycle seed 1 | 19.34 MB / 21.08 dB / 0.517 | 19.45 MB / 22.67 dB / 0.681 | +1.59 |
| bicycle seed 2 | 19.34 MB / 21.07 dB / 0.516 | 19.43 MB / 22.58 dB / 0.680 | +1.51 |
| stump | 15.49 MB / 20.35 dB / 0.391 | 15.12 MB / 23.52 dB / 0.675 | **+3.17** |
| bonsai | 3.94 MB / 24.10 dB / 0.768 | 3.99 MB / 25.74 dB / 0.858 | +1.64 |

T1 PASS, T2 PASS (second seed within 0.08 dB of the first), T4 PASS; **T3 FAIL on stump**: the pre-finetune render is
+0.30 dB on stump while the container outcome is +3.17. The pre-registered mechanism assumption — a better selection
shows up as a better starting point — is wrong: on stump both pruned sets render at 20.6–20.9 dB before training but at
SSIM 0.408 vs 0.726, and over 12k steps the volume set moves −0.02 dB while the saliency set moves +5.00 dB. The score
selects for **trainability**, which the pre-finetune render cannot measure; the pre-finetune row is a diagnostic, never
a criterion (N53).

Mid-rate ratios at 25 % keep: 73.6–77.4× (bicycle 74.0×, stump 77.4×, bonsai 73.6×) — four times the near-lossless
shelf, since pruning and the container are independent axes. The bicycle mid-rate point against PCGS is unchanged
(−2.45 dB); what changed is that the deficit is now demonstrably a *selection* problem on two of three scenes and a
*representation* problem on the best one.
Files: `experiments/M48_RESULTS.md`, `FRONTIER_BOUND.md` **§26**, `sota_table.md` mid-rate three-scene table,
`tools/m48_{spec,drive,one,stump32,gaps,gap2}.sh`, `APPENDIX_negative_results.md` **N53**.
Gates: **159 tests**, 881 byte cases 0 mismatches, 164/164 parse as 3.11, codec selftest PASS.
Cost: node 2141508, 1.6 h ≈ ¥4.3.
## M49 round result (round 32, 2026-11-17 11:30, after the data)

**`calibration_artifact`** — the container costs **0.39 dB** on the saliency-pruned model, not the 1.83 dB M48 implied.
One node, one session, bicycle seed 1: the unquantised 12k-finetuned model (1,532,988 primitives) renders **23.26 dB**
through the container's own probe at factor 1, and the 48-bit container renders **22.87 dB** at 20.56 MB. The number
that produced the 1.83 dB was the factor-4 *training-view* eval (24.75), and the factor-4 render of the same model is
**18.71 dB** — a 4.55 dB protocol gap, three times the effect attributed to quantisation.

Colour sweep on the same model (48 bits is the shipping row; geometry fixed):

| colour bits | file | PSNR | Δ vs raw |
|---|---|---|---|
| raw (no container) | 236 B/prim | 23.26 | — |
| 96 | 30.78 MB | 23.04 | −0.22 |
| 64 | 22.91 MB | 23.01 | −0.25 |
| **48** | **20.56 MB** | **22.87** | **−0.39** |
| 40 | 19.47 MB | 22.63 | −0.63 |
| 32 | 18.35 MB | 21.94 | −1.32 |

C2 therefore returns `geometry_block_dominant`: 48 → 96 bits buys +0.17 dB for +10.22 MB, and the per-primitive
accounting puts geometry at 9.16 of 13.60 B/primitive (**67 %**; corrected 13:20 on 11-17 from the measured stream table). C3 fails: the best row at ≤19.5 MB is
22.63 dB @ 19.47 MB against M48's 22.67 @ 19.45 MB, i.e. the mid-rate point stands within 0.04 dB.

**What this changes.** The mid-rate deficit against PCGS (−2.45 dB) is not a codec deficit: the finetuned model is
already 1.86 dB behind (23.26 raw against 25.12 at 19.09 MB) before any byte is spent. It is a *primitives per byte*
deficit, and two thirds of our bytes per primitive are geometry. Quantisation-aware finetuning is withdrawn as a direction;
the geometry block is the target. Appendix **N54**, `FRONTIER_BOUND.md` §27, `experiments/M49_RESULTS.md`.
Files: `tools/m49_{spec,drive,go}.sh`, `tools/push_m49.ps1`, `experiments/artifacts/a4/m49/`.
Cost: node 2141648, 0.85 h ≈ ¥2.4. Gates: 42/42 parse as 3.11, 881 byte cases 0 mismatches, codec selftest PASS.

## M50 spec: what does a geometry bit cost at mid-rate? (pre-registered 2026-11-17 11:35, before any data)

M49 says two thirds of a mid-rate container is geometry. This round prices that block, one field at a time, on the *same*
finetuned model M49 produced — encode-only, no training, so every row differs only in the geometry code.

**Design.** One model (`/workspace/res/m49/arm_g/point_cloud.ply`, 1,532,988 primitives), colour fixed at 48 bits,
geometry varied over the shipping spec `gp6-6-6_c8_sh3`:

1. `gp6-6-6_c8` (control, = M49's 48-bit row);
2. one field at a time: `gp4-6-6_c8`, `gp6-4-6_c8`, `gp6-6-4_c8`, `gp6-6-6_c7`, `gp6-6-6_c6`;
3. `gp4-4-4_c8` (all three attribute groups coarsened together);
4. `gp4-4-4_c6` (everything coarsened).

Each row: measured `*.gs1` bytes, PSNR and SSIM on the 25 held-out views at factor 1, through the same archive path.

**Criteria.**

* **H1 — the price of a geometry bit.** For each single-field sweep, report `dB per 10 % bytes saved`. PASS if at least
  one field saves **≥ 8 % of the file for ≤ 0.25 dB**, i.e. a byte cheaper than the colour block's marginal rate
  (0.17 dB for 50 % bytes).
* **H2 — the exchange rate.** Compare against the prune exchange rate measured in M17/M18 (a primitive costs ~13 B and
  buys ~0.7 dB at 25 % keep): if H1's best field beats it, spending geometry bytes on primitives wins.
* **H3 — the shipped spec is defended.** If no field clears H1, the round concludes `shipping_spec_is_at_its_rate`
  and the geometry share becomes a property of the representation, not a knob.
* **H4 — gates green** (suite, byte model, py3.11) and every row's bytes are measured file lengths.

**Forbidden.** Re-encoding with a different colour budget in the same comparison; changing the model, seed, views or
steps; quoting the legacy `gp8-8-8` rows as if they were this model.

## M50 round result (round 33, 2026-11-17 11:45, after the data)

**`shipping_spec_is_at_its_rate`** — eight geometry specs on M49's finetuned model (1,532,988 primitives, 48-bit
colour, same 25 held-out views). Control `gp6-6-6_c8`: 20.56 MB / 22.87 dB.

| change | file | PSNR | Δ dB | Δ bytes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| opacity 6 → 4 bits | 20.07 MB | 22.88 | **+0.01** | **−2.37 %** |
| positions 8 → 7 | 19.99 MB | 22.76 | −0.11 | −2.78 % |
| positions 8 → 6 | 19.41 MB | 22.43 | −0.44 | −5.60 % |
| quaternions 6 → 4 | 19.23 MB | 22.58 | −0.29 | −6.48 % |
| scales 6 → 4 | 19.01 MB | 21.96 | −0.91 | −7.54 % |
| quat+scales+opacity 4 | 17.19 MB | 21.65 | −1.22 | −16.40 % |
| + 6-bit positions | 16.04 MB | 21.36 | −1.51 | −22.00 % |

H1 fails (nothing saves ≥ 8 % for ≤ 0.25 dB); H2 fails in the informative direction — geometry knobs cost 0.040–0.121 dB
per 1 % of bytes saved while the M17/M18 primitive exchange rate is 0.013 dB per 1 %, so moving bytes between geometry
precision and primitive count does not pay either way. The mid-rate deficit needs a cheaper geometry *representation*,
not a slider. **One shippable exception: 4-bit opacity is free** (−2.37 % bytes, +0.01 dB, −0.004 SSIM) — the first
free container byte since M34, worth ≈ +0.47× on the near-lossless shelf (76.56 MB → ≈74.7 MB, 18.90× → ≈19.37×) if it
replicates there, which M51 must measure rather than extrapolate.
Files: `tools/m50_drive.sh`, `experiments/M50_RESULTS.md`, `experiments/artifacts/a4/m50/`, `FRONTIER_BOUND.md` §28.
Gates: same node and code as M49 (42/42 parse, 881 byte cases, codec selftest PASS). Cost: 16 min ≈ ¥0.75 on the M49
node, released immediately after the last row.

## M51 spec: does 4-bit opacity hold where it matters? (pre-registered 2026-11-17 11:50, before any data)

M50 measured the free 2.4 % on one pruned model at 48-bit colour. The paper's headline row is a different thing: the
unpruned near-lossless shelf (`gp6-6-6_c9_sh3`, rank 16, 165-bit colour, 76.56 MB / 24.06 dB / 18.90×), measured on
three scenes. This round re-measures that row with opacity at 4 bits and answers whether the byte is free everywhere.

**Design.** The three published models (bicycle 6,131,954 / stump 4,961,797 / bonsai 1,244,819 primitives) from
`/mnt/m35cache`, no training, no pruning. For each scene, encode both:
`gp6-6-6_c8_sh3:16:48` (the shipping near-lossless row, control) and `gp6-6-4_c8_sh3:16:48` (opacity 4 bits).
Each row: measured `*.gs1` bytes, PSNR, SSIM on the held-out views (25 bicycle/stump, 16 bonsai), and decode wall
clock plus peak RSS measured by `m19_codec.py --to-ply` under `/usr/bin/time -v`.

**Correction (2026-11-17 11:55, before any M51 data).** The first draft of this spec named `c9_sh3:16:165`, which is
the M22 colour shelf, not the row the headline quotes. `FRONTIER_BOUND.md` §19/§20 and the 76.56 MB / 18.90× / 24.06 dB
row are `gp6-6-6_c8_sh3` (48-bit colour, 12.49 B/primitive) on the untouched published model. The round tests that row.

**Criteria.**

* **J1 — the byte is free on all three scenes.** PSNR loss ≤ **0.05 dB** and SSIM loss ≤ **0.005** on each scene while
  the file shrinks ≥ **1.5 %**. All three must pass.
* **J2 — the headline ratio moves.** The bicycle shelf ratio improves from 18.90× by at least **+0.3×** at equal PSNR.
* **J3 — decode is unaffected.** Decode wall clock within 2 % and peak RSS within 0.1 GB of the control (the stream
  layout does not change, only its width).
* **J4 — the byte model agrees with the files.** `m1_check_bytes.py` accounts for the new stream widths; a mismatch
  means the container's header still declares 6-bit opacity.

**Forbidden.** Re-tuning anything per scene; reporting a PSNR from a different factor or view set; adopting the spec on
two of three scenes.

## M51 round result (round 34, 2026-11-17 12:55, after the data)

**`not_free_but_cheapest`** — the 4-bit opacity byte M50 found on the pruned model is **not** free on the three
untouched published models; it costs **0.11 dB** on every scene for −2.3…−2.4 % bytes.

| scene | control | opacity 4-bit | Δ bytes | Δ dB | Δ SSIM | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| bicycle | 76,598,203 B / 24.06 | 74,802,867 B / 23.95 | −2.35 % | −0.11 | −0.0063 | 18.90× → 19.35× |
| stump | 62,649,653 B / 25.52 | 61,191,922 B / 25.41 | −2.33 % | −0.11 | −0.0064 | 18.71× → 19.14× |
| bonsai | 15,297,685 B / 27.08 | 14,927,795 B / 26.97 | −2.42 % | −0.11 | −0.0044 | 19.20× → 19.68× |

J1 FAIL (0.11 dB against a 0.05 dB bar), J2 FAIL as written (the ratio moves but not at equal PSNR), J3 PASS (decode
wall within 1.2 %, peak RSS within 0.02 %), J4 PASS for the byte model (the "header still declares 6-bit opacity"
clause unverified). The same 0.11 dB on three scenes with very different primitive counts is a saturation effect, not
noise: pruning had collapsed the opacity distribution, which is why the pruned model tolerated 4 bits.

**It is still the cheapest byte at this end** — 0.047 dB per 1 % against 0.134 for 48 → 40-bit colour and 0.175 for
48 → 32-bit. So the finding is an editorial choice, not a free win: 18.90× at −0.46 dB, or 19.35× at −0.57 dB. The
shipping row stays at 6-bit opacity; the 4-bit variant is documented as the rate knob it is.
Files: `experiments/M51_RESULTS.md`, `tools/m51_{go,fix,pass2}.sh`, `tools/m51_decode.py`,
`experiments/artifacts/a4/m51/`. Cost: node 2141711, 1.08 h ≈ ¥3.0. Appendix **N55**.

## M52 spec: does the shipping container stream? (pre-registered 2026-11-17 13:00, before any data)

Every round so far has decoded the whole file. A container whose streams are independent frames can be cut at a prefix
and still decode; if ours can, that is a deployment property HAC/PCGS do not report, and it costs one render per rung
to measure.

**Design.** The three shipping containers M51 produced (bicycle / stump / bonsai, `gp6-6-6_c8_sh3`, 6-bit opacity),
cut at 25 / 50 / 75 / 100 % of their bytes with `m19_codec.py --ladder --fracs`; each prefix decoded and rendered at
factor 1 on the same held-out views as M51. Report bytes, PSNR, SSIM per rung.

**Criteria.**

* **K1 — monotone and graceful.** PSNR must increase with the prefix on every scene; no single 25 % step may lose more
  than **1.5 dB**, and the 50 % rung must be within **2.0 dB** of the full file.
* **K2 — the first rung is already useful.** At 25 % of the bytes each scene must beat the pruned-mid-rate row *at that
  size* (the M39/M48 curve), i.e. a quarter of the file must be at least as good as pruning to the same budget.
* **K3 — decode cost scales.** Prefix decode wall clock must be ≤ 40 % of full decode at the 25 % rung.
* **K4 — nothing is re-encoded.** Every rung is a byte prefix of the same `*.gs1`; the full-file bytes and PSNR must
  reproduce M51's control rows exactly.

**Forbidden.** Re-encoding a prefix as its own container; reporting a rung whose prefix is not a literal byte cut;
comparing rungs across scenes with different view counts without saying so (bicycle/stump 25 views, bonsai 16).

## M52 spec: at 19 MB, is 1.53 M primitives the right place to sit? (pre-registered 2026-11-17 13:25, before any data)

**Revision notice, written before any M52 data.** The previous pre-registration (13:00) made M52 the streaming-decode
round (criteria K1–K4). That is deferred to M53: M49/M50/M51 changed the question. Two thirds of a mid-rate container is
geometry (9.16 of 13.60 B/primitive) and the colour block is 4.34 B, so at a fixed 19 MB the pipeline can trade colour
bits for primitives — and nobody has measured where that trade bottoms out. This round measures it.

## Why it matters

Our best mid-rate row is 1.53 M primitives at 48-bit colour: 19.47 MB / 22.63 dB. PCGS reports 25.12 dB at 19.09 MB.
If the optimum at equal bytes is a *different* (keep, colour) pair, then the 2.5 dB gap is partly a mis-allocation we can
fix inside the current representation; if the control is already the optimum, every remaining decibel needs a different
representation and the paper's honest claim is "near-lossless, not mid-rate".

## Design (bicycle, saliency score, seed 1, 12k finetune — M47/M48 recipe unchanged)

Three keep fractions, each pruned by the saliency vector measured once (one 800-step pass), finetuned 12k steps, then
encoded at the colour budgets that land near 19.5 MB:

| arm | keep | primitives | colour budgets encoded |
|---|---|---|---|
| A (control) | 25 % | 1,532,988 | 48, 40 (M49 already has both) |
| B | 40 % | 2,453,182 | 32, 24 |
| C | 55 % | 3,372,375 | 24, 16 |

Every row: measured `*.gs1` bytes, PSNR and SSIM on the 25 held-out views at factor 1. Equal-bytes comparison window
is **19.0–20.0 MB**; a row outside it is reported but not compared.

## Criteria

* **E1 — the optimum moves.** Best row inside the equal-bytes window beats M49's 22.63 dB by **≥ 0.30 dB** →
  `allocation_matters`; otherwise `one_point_five_million_is_the_optimum`.
* **E2 — the slopes are reported, not just the winner.** dB per extra million primitives (at matched colour) and dB per
  colour bit (at matched keep), both fitted over the measured rows only.
* **E3 — equal bytes means equal bytes.** The compared rows must be within 1.0 % of each other in measured file size,
  and the loss must be stated in dB against the same clean baseline.
* **E4 — gates green.** Byte model, py3.11 parse, codec selftest on the round's node.

## Forbidden

* Tuning the finetune recipe, the saliency steps or the seed per arm.
* Comparing a row at 18 MB with one at 21 MB as if the rates matched.
* Reporting a colour budget that was not actually encoded (the `*.gs1` length is the rate).

### M52 revision (2026-11-17 13:40, before any data)

The 13:25 design (keep 25/40/55 % at colour budgets landing near 19.5 MB) is not executable: geometry alone costs
9.16 B/primitive, so 2.45 M primitives cannot fit in 19.5 MB at any colour budget (floor ≈ 22.5 MB) and 3.37 M cannot
come near it. The arms would have measured the arithmetic. Revised design — one corner, chosen because it is the only
one the floor leaves open:

| arm | keep | primitives | spec | colour | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A (control) | 25 % | 1,532,988 | `gp6-6-6_c8_sh3` | 40 | M49's equal-bytes point, re-encoded in this session |
| B1 | 43 % | 2,636,640 | `gp4-4-4_c6_sh3` | 16 | the coarse-geometry + more-primitives corner |
| B2 | 43 % | 2,636,640 | `gp6-6-6_c8_sh3` | 32 | the same primitives with the shipping geometry, to separate the two effects |

Criteria unchanged in form: **E1** best row inside 19.0–20.0 MB beats 22.63 dB by ≥ 0.30 dB (`allocation_matters`), else
`the_floor_decides`; **E2** report dB per million primitives and dB per colour bit from these rows; **E3** equal bytes
within 1 %; **E4** gates green. If B1 or B2 lands outside the window, it is reported and the window comparison is made
between whichever two rows do fall inside it.

## M52 outcome (round 35, 2026-11-17 13:55): analysis, not a node measurement

The round was pre-registered as the equal-bytes allocation test (13:25) and revised (13:40) when the geometry floor made
its arms arithmetically impossible. It then could not be run: 16区 A100 was sold out all afternoon, and the 1区 A40
attempt was aborted — `download.pytorch.org` throttled to ~110 kB/s (528 MB LPIPS backbone = 80 min) and the HF model
pull ran at ~180 kB/s (1.52 GB = 2.3 h), against ~3 min in 16区. Cost of the aborted attempt: 44 min ≈ ¥2.1, released.

Instead of a slow round, the question was closed with a bound computed from measurements already on disk:
`experiments/MIDRATE_BOUND.md`.

**Result — the mid-rate allocation axis is closed, quantitatively.** Selling geometry bytes costs **0.30–0.90 dB per
byte** (M50/M51 knobs); buying primitives with those bytes pays **0.075 dB per byte** (M18's exchange). The full
coarsening `gp6-6-6_c8 → gp4-4-4_c6` saves 2.95 B/primitive = +28.2 % primitives = +0.40 dB, and costs 1.51 dB:
**net −1.11 dB**. Every allocation the current representation can reach loses by a factor of 4–12 in dB per byte.

**What that means for the 2.5 dB.** To carry 2.8 M primitives in 19.5 MB, geometry must reach 4.0 B/primitive; at the
measured price that costs 1.5–4.6 dB while the extra primitives buy +0.9 dB. A byte-shaving representation therefore
makes the mid-rate worse unless the *same* bytes become cheaper in distortion — i.e. unless the code is trained
through (anchor/VQ), which is exactly what PCGS does and what post-hoc pruning cannot. The mid-rate wall is a
**reconstruction-cost wall**, not a physical limit and not an allocation error.

**Falsifiable target for the anchor round** (to be pre-registered if it is run): geometry at 4.0 B/primitive with
≤ 0.6 dB loss — 0.15 dB/B, twice as good as the best knob measured so far — nets +0.3 dB at equal bytes. Anything above
0.15 dB/B is arithmetic that loses.

**Operational rule added.** Rent 16区 A100 only; if sold out, run an analysis round rather than a slow one. `m52_go.sh`
now caches COLMAP and the model PLY to `/mnt` so staging is paid once per region, and `probe_render_psnr.sh` renders
without `--fidelity` so rounds that do not need SSIM/LPIPS do not pay the 528 MB download.

## M53 spec: anchor-conditioned prediction of the attribute block (pre-registered 2026-11-17 14:15, before any data)

**Why this round exists.** §30/§31 closed the allocation axis: coarsening geometry to buy primitives loses 0.6–1.1 dB,
and the attributes alone (5.88 B/primitive) exceed the whole geometry budget an anchor scheme could hope for. The only
remaining mid-rate lever is **prediction**: keep the attributes expensive to *represent* but cheap to *code*, by
predicting each primitive's quaternion, scale and opacity from its anchor and coding the residual.

**Design (bicycle first; the scorer is the round's own prototype).**

1. Anchors: grid cells over the scene at a cell size of k × the median primitive spacing (k ∈ {2, 4}), one anchor per
   occupied cell, anchor position quantised to 16 bits/axis. Primitives assigned to the nearest occupied cell.
2. Prediction, two candidates measured side by side:
   * **P0 (control):** anchor mean of the three attribute groups (no training).
   * **P1:** ridge regression from the anchor's feature vector (mean quaternion, mean log-scale, mean opacity, anchor
     position, cell occupancy) to each primitive's attributes, fitted on half the primitives, applied to all.
3. Residual coding: quantise residuals to the width that the shipping container uses for the same field, zstd at level
   12, measured `*.gs1`-style byte counts per stream. **Positions are coded as anchor index + 4-bit offsets** in both
   arms, so the comparison isolates prediction.
4. Render: reconstructed model written as a PLY, archived through `make_m360_archive.py`, rendered at factor 1 on the 25
   held-out views. Every arm is compared against the same M49/M50 control model (23.26 dB uncompressed).

**Criteria.**

* **R1 — the bar.** Geometry ≤ **2.8 B/primitive** at ≤ **0.4 dB** loss against the uncompressed control. Both clauses
  must hold; either alone is arithmetic that loses (§31).
* **R2 — prediction beats the anchor mean.** P1's residual entropy must be at least **1.0 B/primitive** below P0's, or
  the prediction carries no information the mean does not.
* **R3 — the equal-bytes consequence.** If R1 holds, encode the reconstructed model at 19.5 MB and report its PSNR
  against 22.63 dB; if R1 fails, stop and record `prediction_cannot_pay_for_the_anchor`.
* **R4 — gates green** (byte model, py3.11, codec selftest).

**Forbidden.** Reporting a bytes-per-primitive figure that excludes the anchor stream or the anchor indices; training
P1 on the same primitives it is scored on without saying so; comparing against a different control model than the
23.26 dB one.

**Status.** Not run: 16区 A100 was sold out at 13:00 and again at 14:05 (three pools, zero units). The prototype is the
next round's first task; it runs on one node in ~40 min once the code exists.

### M53 status (round 36, 2026-11-17 13:55): prototype delivered, node run blocked on A100 stock

16区 A100 was sold out at 13:00, 13:40 and 14:20 (three pools, zero units each time), so the round could not measure
on data. What it did deliver is the thing the next node minute would otherwise be spent building:

* `experiments/m53_anchor.py` — the anchor + prediction prototype. Positions become an anchor index plus
  `offbits`-wide offsets in the cell; quaternions, scales and opacity are predicted from anchor features **and the
  primitive's own decoder-side position**, fitted by ridge on the even-indexed primitives and applied to all of them.
  It reports the shipping-style byte count per stream, the order-0 entropy bound per stream, the reconstruction RMSE,
  and writes the reconstructed model back as an INRIA PLY so the round can render it.
* `experiments/tests/test_m53_anchor.py` — three tests: the stream sizes sum to the reported total, the position error
  obeys the quantisation step, and the predictor beats the anchor mean *only* when the attributes depend on position
  (a predictor that "wins" on independent data would be an accounting bug).
* end-to-end PLY round trip verified locally (`save_inria_ply` → prototype → `load_inria_ply`).

`pytest experiments/tests` is **162 passed** (159 before this round). The prototype's synthetic selfcheck is a
worst case (random attributes, no structure) and reports 11 B/primitive — it is not a result, it is a smoke test. The
number that decides M53's R1 (≤ 2.8 B/primitive at ≤ 0.4 dB) needs the published bicycle PLY, which needs a node.

**Next round's first command, once a 16区 A100 exists:**

```bash
bash tools/m53_go.sh     # prototype on /mnt/m35cache/bicycle.ply at k in {2,4} x offbits in {4,3} x mode {p0,p1},
                         # then archive + render each reconstruction at factor 1 and print the bytes/dB table
```

Files added this round: `experiments/m53_anchor.py`, `experiments/tests/test_m53_anchor.py`, and `tools/m53_go.sh`
(the one-shot driver: deps, cached data, control render, eight prototype settings, archive + render per setting).

## M54 audit: every number in the last three rounds is backed by its artifact (round 37, 2026-11-17 14:10)

A100 still sold out (three pools, zero units, fourth check today), so the round audited instead of renting. The failure
it targets is the one M49 caught in its own predecessor: a number that is right in the artifact and wrong in the prose.

`tools/audit_mseries_tables.py` reads the artifact rows of M49/M50/M51 and requires every row's **measured byte count**
and **PSNR to two decimals** to appear in the matching `*_RESULTS.md`, and flags byte figures in the prose that no
artifact row backs.

| round | artifact rows | result |
|---|---|---|
| M49 | 5 (b32/40/48/64/96 + raw) | all present, bytes + PSNR + MB |
| M50 | 8 (eight geometry specs) | all present, bytes + PSNR + MB |
| M51 | 6 (three scenes × two opacity widths) | all present, bytes + PSNR; no MB figures in that table by design |

`pytest experiments/tests/test_audit_mseries.py` (2 tests) pins it, including the row-count per round so a lost
artifact fails the suite: **164 passed** total. The audit's JSON is `experiments/artifacts/a4/audit_m49_51.json`.

**Not yet covered, stated rather than implied:** M47 and M48. Their artifacts are per-arm logs
(`fetch/<scene>/enc_*.log` for MB, `render_*.log` for PSNR) rather than rows files, and the write-ups quote
*equal-bytes pairs* that need two logs matched per claim. That is the next extension, and until it lands the M47/M48
tables should be treated as audited-by-eye only.

## M55 protocol-asset registry: bonsai is excluded, mechanically (round 38, 2026-11-17 14:35)

A100 sold out for the fifth check today. The round closed a hole that had already produced a wrong claim: M16 established
that `bicycle` and `stump` reproduce the published 3DGS numbers within the measured 0.55 dB protocol bias, and that
`bonsai` does **not** (our render 28.06 against a published 32.12 — 4.1 dB), so bonsai rows are internal comparisons
only. That rule lived in one paragraph of M16 and drifted: this session's own summary compared our bonsai row against
PCGS's 33.20 as if the two were on the same protocol, which is exactly the invalid comparison M16 refused to make.

Now the rule is code and CI:

* `experiments/protocol_assets.py` — the registry: per scene, whether it is cross-paper comparable, our clean PSNR, the
  published 3DGS PSNR, and the reason. `sanity()` refuses a registry that marks a scene comparable while the deltas say
  otherwise.
* `tools/lint_protocol_claims.py` — flags any line that names an excluded scene **and** a published method **and** a
  PSNR, unless the line states the exclusion. Runs over the twelve documents that carry numbers.
* `experiments/tests/test_protocol_assets.py` — four tests: registry consistency, a positive case (two flagged lines),
  a qualified statement (clean), and the default document set (clean today).

**What this changes for the paper.** Cross-paper quantitative claims are now restricted to **bicycle and stump**. Bonsai
stays in the paper for within-pipeline comparisons (saliency vs volume, opacity width, damage locality) and must be
labelled as such. With bonsai out, the cross-paper evidence is two scenes, not three — that is the honest count and it
belongs in the abstract, not in a footnote.

**Still open, stated rather than implied.** The 4.1 dB bonsai gap has never been explained: the checkpoint's primitive
count (1,244,819) matches the official one, and bicycle/stump match to 0.6 dB on the same renderer, so the suspicion is
the GT/COLMAP pairing for bonsai rather than the model. Resolving it would put bonsai back into the cross-paper set and
would take one control training plus a standard-split render — a 30-minute node round whenever A100 stock returns.

### M53 amendment: the attribute block also gets a joint VQ arm (round 42, 2026-11-17 15:25, before any data)

Still no A100 (ninth check). The prototype gained the mechanism the arithmetic says is needed: `encode_geometry(...,
vq_k=K)` now vector-quantises the *joint* (quaternion, log-scale, logit-opacity) residual — one code per primitive
plus a shared codebook — instead of coding eight scalars. Mini-batch k-means fits on a 20 k sample and assigns in
chunks, so a 6 M-primitive model stays inside a few hundred MB.

Synthetic selfcheck (random attributes, worst case, 4 000 primitives — a smoke test, not a result):

| arm | attribute bytes | geometry bytes |
|---|---|---|
| P0, scalar | 7.37 B/prim | 10.98 B/prim |
| P1, scalar | 7.45 | 11.06 |
| **P1 + VQ-64** | **1.25** | **4.86** |
| P1 + VQ-256 | 2.95 | 6.56 |

The K=256 arm is worse *on 4 000 primitives* because an 8 KB codebook costs 2 B/prim at that size; at 1.5 M primitives
it costs 0.005 B/prim. That is exactly the kind of arithmetic the node run has to replace with a measurement.

Criteria unchanged, with the VQ arm added to the grid: R1 (≤ 2.8 B/primitive geometry at ≤ 0.4 dB) is now tested on
`(k, offbits, mode, vq_k)` with `vq_k ∈ {0, 64, 256}`; R2 (prediction beats the anchor mean by ≥ 1.0 B/primitive) is
computed on the same rows. `pytest experiments/tests` is **181 passed** (two new tests: joint VQ beats the scalar path
and its byte accounting adds up; VQ error falls with codebook size).

### M53 scale bench: the prototype survives 1.5 M primitives in 37 s (round 43, 2026-11-17 15:40)

A100 sold out for the tenth check. Rather than write the rebuild plan, the round removed the risk that would waste the
next rented GPU: does `encode_geometry` run at the published model's size on CPU?

`tools/m53_bench.py` builds a clustered synthetic model at an arbitrary primitive count (anchor-dependent attributes,
so P1 has something to learn) and reports wall clock and bytes per stage.

| n | arm | time | geometry | positions | attributes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 400,000 | scalar | 1.8 s | 8.79 B/prim | 2.72 | 6.07 |
| 400,000 | VQ-64 | 4.3 s | **3.47** | 2.72 | **0.76** |
| 1,532,988 | scalar | 6.4 s | 8.63 | 2.69 | 5.95 |
| 1,532,988 | **VQ-256** | **37.2 s** | **3.69** | 2.69 | **1.01** |

Three facts worth carrying into the node round:

* **Runtime is not a risk.** 37 s for 1.53 M primitives including a 256-entry codebook fit; the node budget is dominated
  by data staging, not by the prototype.
* **Peak memory is not a risk either**, with the chunked assignment; the earlier single-pass version would have needed
  ~3.3 GB just for one 200 k-primitive block against 256 centroids.
* **The synthetic number is not a result.** 3.69 B/primitive sits just above the 2.8 B bar *on data whose attributes
  were generated from the anchor by construction* — the easiest possible case for prediction. A real scene will be worse,
  and the node run is what decides R1.

`pytest experiments/tests` is **182 passed** (one new test: chunked VQ assignment must equal a single pass, so the
memory fix cannot silently change the codes).

## M59 the rebuild plan, with gates and a kill switch (round 44, 2026-11-17 16:00)

Eleventh stock check: 16区 A100 went from 1 available to 0 between two calls, A40 is 0 in all four pools, and the only

burn money to fail at import. No node this round either.

The round wrote `experiments/PLAN_REPRESENTATION.md`: what a representation rebuild must contain, what it costs, and the
four gates that end it early if the arithmetic does not hold.

* **G1 (passed)** prototype runs at 1.53 M primitives, 37 s on CPU.
* **G2 (next, ¥2, 40 min)** on a real PLY: geometry ≤ 2.8 B/primitive at ≤ 0.4 dB. Fails → `prediction_cannot_pay_for_the_anchor`,

* **G3 (≈¥25)** trained through the code: equal-bytes point beats 22.67 dB by ≥ 0.3 dB. Fails → `training_does_not_close_it`.

## M53 round result (round 45, 2026-11-17 16:10): the anchor prototype is cheap and (currently) invalid

First node in eleven attempts: 16区 A100 sold out all day, so the round ran on an **RTX 4090 in 亚太2区** (¥2.20/h,
14:35–16:05 ≈ ¥3.3). Data staging took 8 minutes at 28 MB/s from hf-mirror, against 180 kB/s in 1区 — 4090/亚太2区 is
the working fallback while A100 is unavailable.

**Byte side works and clears the bar.** After sorting primitives by anchor (the model-order version spent 7.7 B/primitive
on the anchor-index stream alone), geometry costs **2.15 B/primitive** with joint VQ-64: positions 1.43, attributes 0.72,
against the shipping container's 9.16. VQ-256 gives 2.39; scalar attributes 7.51.

**Distortion side is broken.** Control (untouched published model through the same probe) renders 24.52 dB; every
reconstruction renders 10.9–12.4 dB. Ruled out this round: the PLY writer (an identity round trip renders 24.52), the
colour permutation (a real bug — the prototype sorted primitives and wrote SH in the original order — fixed, −12.7 →
−12.1 dB), the attribute budget (12-bit near-lossless attributes change the render by 0.00 dB), the position step
(RMSE 0.033 against a 0.063 offset step), and the marginal distributions (means/scales/opacities match at p1/p50/p99).

**Verdict `prototype_not_valid`, R1 undecided.** R1 (≤2.8 B/primitive at ≤0.4 dB) is not met, but the failure is a
defect in the prototype, not evidence about anchors, so `PLAN_REPRESENTATION.md`'s kill rule must **not** fire. The next
attempt starts with a rendered-pixel unit test on a 200-primitive scene, zeroing one field at a time; the two candidates
this round did not isolate are the quaternion component sign/order and the opacity logit/sigmoid round trip.

**Process failures.** The node was released before the artifact bundle was downloaded, so the round's logs are not on
disk; every number above is from in-session reads. Fetch the bundle, then release. `tail` is unusable through this
harness on these images and a `python | grep` script swallows its own failure — both cost node time and are replaced by
`sed`/`awk` and direct redirection in the round's scripts.

## M53 addendum: two real bugs in the prototype, and the design flaw underneath (round 46, 2026-11-17 16:25)

No 4090 left when the round tried to rent (the unit listed at 16:09 was gone by 16:10 — third time today), so the round
worked on the 12 dB defect with CPU experiments on the local model generator. It found the defect's mechanism.

**Bug 1 — offsets were coded around the cell *mean*.** Primitives sit anywhere inside their cell, and a primitive near a
cell corner is up to 0.87·cell from the mean, while the offset code can only express ±0.5·cell. On a bicycle-like
generator, **1 % of primitives were displaced by up to 1.42 world units** — with median position error 0.1, which is why
means and percentiles looked healthy while the render collapsed. Anchoring at the cell **centre** (and keeping the mean
for attribute prediction only) removes the clipping: max error 1.42 → 0.18, and the ">3x median" tail goes 0.99 % → 0.00 %.

**Bug 2 — the spacing estimator measured the wrong distance.** `estimate_spacing` took the median nearest-neighbour
distance on a 4 000-point random subsample. On clustered data a subsample point's nearest sampled neighbour is almost
never in its own cluster, so it measured the *inter-cluster* distance: **1.05 versus a true ~0.06** on the test scene,
which made the cell 20× too large and the offsets 20× too coarse. Replaced with a density estimate (0.554*lambda^(-1/3)，
O(n), no subsample bias). It fixed the uniform case but is itself biased on clustered data (0.71 against 0.06), so it is
better, not correct.

**The design flaw underneath.** Both estimators assume a spatially uniform point set. A 3DGS model is a set of surfaces
plus floaters: neither a global density nor a random-subsample NN describes it. The shipping container avoids the whole
problem by clustering **by count** (256 primitives per cluster in Morton order) rather than by a spatial grid. The
prototype's grid anchors should be replaced by the container's own count-based layout — same anchor, same offset idea,
but with a grouping that matches how 3DGS data is distributed.

`pytest experiments/tests` is **182 passed**; the round's numbers come from the local generator
(`tools/m53_bench.py`'s scene, 200 k primitives), not from a node.

## M53 addendum 2: count-based clusters, and the measurement they force (round 47, 2026-11-17 16:45)

The 4090 and A30 listed at 16:30 were both gone before a rent could confirm (fourth and fifth time today), so the round
did the code work the previous round concluded was needed and measured what it could on the CPU.

**Count-based clusters implemented.** `build_clusters(means, group)` sorts primitives along a Morton curve and cuts
every `group` of them into one cluster, coding offsets inside the cluster's own bounding box — the shipping container's
layout, with the per-primitive anchor index removed entirely (it is implied by stream position). `encode_geometry(...,
group=256)` selects it; `group=0` keeps the spatial grid.

**What that measures, on the same 200 k-primitive clustered scene, 8-bit offsets, VQ-64 attributes:**

| layout | geometry B/prim | positions | attributes | position error mean / max |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| spatial grid, k=3 | 3.93 | 3.17 | 0.76 | 0.004 / 0.007 |
| count clusters, 256 | 3.80 | 3.04 | 0.76 | 0.014 / 0.177 |
| count clusters, 64 | 3.86 | 3.10 | 0.76 | 0.008 / 0.168 |

The position block costs **3.0–3.2 B/primitive in every layout** — the same as the shipping container's 3.38. Clustering
by count removes the anchor-id stream but a Morton chunk's box is not compact, so a fixed 8-bit offset over it is
coarser (max error 0.18 against 0.007), exactly the trade the container also makes.

**The conclusion this forces.** Positions cannot be made cheaper by grouping: at equal error they cost what the container
already spends. So the entire remaining question is the **attribute block** — 0.76 B/primitive with VQ-64 against the
container's 4.34 — and the only thing that decides it is the render at that budget, on a real model. That is one node
measurement (bonsai, ~40 min staging on a slow region, 15 min of compute, ≈¥2), and the driver is written and pushed
(`tools/m53_bonsai.sh <group> <offbits>` → three VQ arms plus a control).

`pytest experiments/tests` is **182 passed**.

## M53 G2 verdict (round 49, 2026-11-17 17:25): `prediction_cannot_pay_for_the_anchor`

Measured on bonsai's published checkpoint, CPU only (every usable GPU pool was empty; the 1区 pools reject the CUDA 12.4
image). With the container's own count-based clustering and the prototype's ridge predictor:

* residuals after prediction: quat 0.225, log-scale 1.36, logit-opacity 3.80 RMS;
* scalar 6-bit coding of the same residuals: 0.0092 / 0.094 / 0.096 — i.e. the container's approach is ~6x more accurate
  at 4.3 B/primitive than joint VQ-64 is at 1.01 B/primitive (RMS 0.55);
* VQ-256 (1.03 B) reaches 0.43, VQ-1024 (2.11 B) reaches 0.35 — still far above the scalar path.

Since the container loses only 0.39 dB at 4.3 B/primitive, an attribute error six to eight times larger cannot meet R1's
0.4 dB. **G2 fails and the pre-registered kill rule fires**: the anchor-plus-prediction rebuild stops. The synthetic
"0.72 B/primitive" result was an artefact of generating attributes from the anchor.

The only surviving branch is a trained predictor (G3), and M52's arithmetic still caps its payoff at ~+0.9 dB from the
extra primitives alone. Recommendation: **do not fund G3** without an external reason; finish the compression line as
"near-lossless + robustness + audited negative results", which is what the evidence supports.

`pytest experiments/tests` 184 passed; `experiments/M53_RESULTS.md` carries the full table.

## M61 same-model baselines: the harness, and the byte side measured locally (round 51, 2026-11-17 19:20)

The literature check made one gap unavoidable: every cross-paper number this project owns compares *our* post-hoc
compression of a published checkpoint against HAC/PCGS numbers from models **they retrained**. No reviewer will accept
that as a method comparison, and no same-model baseline had ever been run. This round built the harness for it.

`tools/same_model_baselines.sh <scene> <keep> [fisher]` runs, from one checkpoint and one container spec: the untouched
control, opacity pruning (the viewer default and LightGaussian's starting point), volume pruning (this project's old
proxy), area pruning, and — with `fisher` — the accumulated-gradient score. The first four need no GPU beyond rendering;
the Fisher arm needs one 800-step pass.

**Byte side measured locally on bonsai** (published checkpoint, 1,244,819 primitives, container `gp6-6-6_c8_sh3`,
48-bit colour):

| arm | primitives | file | B/primitive |
|---|---|---|---|
| control (no pruning) | 1,244,819 | 15.30 MB | 12.29 |
| opacity, keep 50 % | 622,410 | 7.95 MB | 12.77 |
| volume, keep 50 % | 622,410 | 7.84 MB | 12.59 |
| area, keep 50 % | 622,410 | 7.84 MB | 12.59 |

Pruning to half the primitives halves the file, as it must; the arms differ by only 1.4 % in bytes, so the comparison at
equal bytes is fair to within noise. **The PSNR column is what decides it and needs a GPU render** — the script writes
`rows.txt` with a `NA` there until one exists.

**A finding that changes the project's own claim.** The "saliency" score of M47/M48 is accumulated squared gradients of
means/scales/opacity per primitive — that is a Fisher-diagonal score, i.e. the PUP-3DGS idea. So M47's +1.6…+3.2 dB is a
*replication* of a published score in a new setting (compression), not a new method, and the baseline harness now makes
that explicit by listing `fisher` beside the other scores. The paper must present it that way.

## M62 round result (round 52, 2026-11-17 20:45): the same-model baseline table exists, and it is less flattering than the old one

Node 2142164 (A100, ¥2.80/h, 19:45–20:45 ≈ ¥2.8), one checkpoint per scene, five scores, one container, equal bytes.

| score | bonsai (50 % keep, control 27.08 dB) | bicycle (50 % keep, control 24.06 dB) |
|---|---|---|
| fisher (our saliency / PUP score) | 8.07 MB / **26.58** (−0.50) | 40.17 MB / **23.53** (−0.53) |
| opacity (viewer default) | 7.95 MB / 26.15 (−0.93) | 39.50 MB / 22.78 (−1.28) |
| area | 7.84 MB / 24.92 (−2.16) | 39.00 MB / 22.14 (−1.92) |
| volume (this project's old proxy) | 7.84 MB / 24.55 (−2.53) | 38.76 MB / 19.67 (−4.39) |

**What it establishes.** (a) A same-model baseline table now exists, which is the comparison reviewers can check; both
controls reproduce the M51 containers to the byte (76,598,203 B / 24.06 dB and 15,297,685 B / 27.08 dB). (b) The Fisher
score loses a stable **0.5 dB for half the bytes** on both scenes. (c) The gain the project has been quoting — +2.0…+3.9 dB
— is over the `volume` proxy, and on bicycle that proxy costs 4.4 dB, so the old comparison flattered the method.
(d) Against `opacity`, the score a practitioner would actually use, the gain is **+0.43…+0.75 dB**.

**What it forces in the paper.** The saliency result must be written as a replication and quantification of the
Fisher/PUP score inside a compression pipeline — not as a new score — and the headline should be the 0.5 dB cost of
halving the file, with the score comparison as the supporting table.

Files: `tools/same_model_baselines.sh`, `experiments/M62_RESULTS.md`, `experiments/artifacts/a4/smb_{bonsai,bicycle}/`.

## M63 round result (round 53, 2026-11-17 22:35): BD-rate says the two best pruning scores are the same score

Node 2142199 (A100, ¥2.80/h, 20:50–22:30 ≈ ¥4.6). bicycle, keep 50 %, no finetuning, four colour budgets per arm plus a
24-bit control point so the curves overlap; BD-rate by piecewise-linear integration over the common quality range
(`tools/bd_rate.py`, self-tested on synthetic shifts of −50 % and +25 %).

| arm | BD-rate vs control | reading |
|---|---|---|
| fisher (PUP/Fisher score) | **−45.06 %** | +0.84 dB at equal rate near 60 MB; highest ceiling (23.75 dB) |
| opacity | **−44.96 %** | statistically the same BD-rate |
| volume | undefined | whole curve 4–4.4 dB below the control's range |

**Three corrections to the project's own narrative.** (1) Pruning to half is worth ~45 % of the bitrate with no finetuning
— that is the strong, defensible number. (2) The Fisher and opacity scores are *the same score* as far as rate is
concerned; their difference is a quality ceiling (+0.8 dB), not a curve shift. (3) The "+2.0…+3.9 dB over volume" that the
last six rounds quoted is a comparison against a broken arm and should be retired from the abstract.

**LPIPS did not materialise**: `--fidelity --lpips-net vgg` runs (the backbone downloaded in 42 s) but `qfp_rd` writes
`"lpips": null` into its manifest for every entry, so the column is `NA`. That is a code-path question for a later round,
not a measurement.

Files: `experiments/M63_RESULTS.md`, `tools/smb_rates.sh`, `tools/bd_rate.py`, `experiments/artifacts/a4/smb_rates/`.

## M64 LPIPS: found, fixed, and verified (round 54, 2026-11-17 22:55)

Node 2142269 (A100, ≈25 min ≈ ¥1.2). The M63 LPIPS column was `NA` for two independent reasons, both now fixed and
verified end to end:

1. **The backbone was never downloaded by the harness.** `dump_artifacts._fidelity_stats` refuses to load LPIPS when the
   weight file is absent (deliberate: a hidden 528 MB download can stall a campaign). `smb_rates.sh`, `same_model_baselines.sh`
   and `lpips_check.sh` now warm it explicitly (`lpips.LPIPS(net='vgg')`) before probing.
2. **The check looked in one directory.** It now searches `$TORCH_HOME`, `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/torch`, `~/.cache/torch` and
   `/root/.cache/torch`, de-duplicated, and the error message names every path tried
   (`experiments/tests/test_lpips_paths.py`, 3 tests).
3. **A 6.1 M-primitive render leaves no room for VGG.** The renderer holds ~32 GB of the 40 GB card, so the 6 GB backbone
   OOMs; `_fidelity_stats` now catches `torch.cuda.OutOfMemoryError`, moves the net to CPU and retries — ~40 s for 25
   full-resolution views.

**Verified**: bicycle control, `--fidelity --lpips-net vgg` → PSNR 24.52, SSIM 0.7705, **LPIPS 0.2397** in the manifest
(previously `null`). For reference the 3DGS paper reports LPIPS ≈ 0.245 at 25.11 dB on the same scene, so the number is
in the right place.

`pytest experiments/tests` **187 passed**. The M63 table's LPIPS column still needs one re-probe of the sixteen archive
entries (~80 min of node time) — the fix is in place, the column is not yet filled.

## M64 (round 55, 2026-11-18 00:10): the LPIPS column is filled, and it agrees with the PSNR ordering

Node 2142294 (A100, 23:18–00:10 ≈ ¥2.4, of which the first half was wasted on a partial bonsai image set — see the
harness bug below). bicycle, keep 50 %, 48-bit colour, probe with `--fidelity --lpips-net vgg`:

| arm | file | PSNR | SSIM | LPIPS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| control | 76.60 MB | **24.06** | 0.7393 | **0.2813** |
| fisher (PUF/Fisher score) | 40.17 MB | 23.52 | 0.7269 | **0.2916** |
| opacity | 39.50 MB | 22.78 | 0.7049 | 0.3034 |
| area | 39.00 MB | 22.14 | 0.6662 | 0.3264 |
| volume | 38.76 MB | 19.67 | 0.5782 | 0.3777 |

Reading: half the bytes costs **+0.010 LPIPS** with the Fisher score and **+0.096** with the volume proxy — the same
ordering as PSNR and SSIM, so the score choice is not an artefact of one metric. The control row reproduces M62's
76,598,203 B / 24.06 dB exactly.

## A harness bug worth recording

The first bisect of this round ran `same_model_baselines.sh bonsai` against a **partial image set (34 of 292 images**:
`fetch_m360_colmap.sh` had been interrupted, and the script's guard only checked that `images_4/` *existed*). The archive
built from 34 images rendered 5 views and reported 26.59 dB instead of 27.08 — a 0.5 dB swing that looks exactly like a
protocol change. The guard must count images against the scene's own list, not test for the directory. Then a *forced*
refetch returned **0** images, so the bosai mirror is currently unreliable; bicycle (194/194) was clean and is the scene
the paper leads with.

`pytest experiments/tests` 187 passed. Bundle: `experiments/artifacts/a4/smb_bicycle_lpips/` (rows, stage log, manifest
with the per-arm lpips values).
